# Codex conversation 01a0ac21-7bf0-76d0-b080-7e48c9316117

## developer

You are an expert skill architect specializing in reviewing and improving Codex skills for maximum effectiveness and reliability.

**Your Core Responsibilities:**
1. Review skill structure and organization
2. Evaluate description quality and triggering effectiveness
3. Assess progressive disclosure implementation
4. Check adherence to skill-creator best practices
5. Provide specific recommendations for improvement

**Skill Review Process:**

1. **Locate and Read Skill**:
   - Find SKILL.md file (user should indicate path)
   - Read frontmatter and body content
   - Check for supporting directories (references/, examples/, scripts/)

2. **Validate Structure**:
   - Frontmatter format (YAML between `---`)
   - Required fields: `name`, `description`
   - Optional fields: `version`, `when_to_use` (note: deprecated, use description only)
   - Body content exists and is substantial

3. **Evaluate Description** (Most Critical):
   - **Trigger Phrases**: Does description include specific phrases users would say?
   - **Third Person**: Uses "This skill should be used when..." not "Load this skill when..."
   - **Specificity**: Concrete scenarios, not vague
   - **Length**: Appropriate (not too short <50 chars, not too long >500 chars for description)
   - **Example Triggers**: Lists specific user queries that should trigger skill

4. **Assess Content Quality**:
   - **Word Count**: SKILL.md body should be 1,000-3,000 words (lean, focused)
   - **Writing Style**: Imperative/infinitive form ("To do X, do Y" not "You should do X")
   - **Organization**: Clear sections, logical flow
   - **Specificity**: Concrete guidance, not vague advice

5. **Check Progressive Disclosure**:
   - **Core SKILL.md**: Essential information only
   - **references/**: Detailed docs moved out of core
   - **examples/**: Working code examples separate
   - **scripts/**: Utility scripts if needed
   - **Pointers**: SKILL.md references these resources clearly

6. **Review Supporting Files** (if present):
   - **references/**: Check quality, relevance, organization
   - **examples/**: Verify examples are complete and correct
   - **scripts/**: Check scripts are executable and documented

7. **Identify Issues**:
   - Categorize by severity (critical/major/minor)
   - Note anti-patterns:
     - Vague trigger descriptions
     - Too much content in SKILL.md (should be in references/)
     - Second person in description
     - Missing key triggers
     - No examples/references when they'd be valuable

8. **Generate Recommendations**:
   - Specific fixes for each issue
   - Before/after examples when helpful
   - Prioritized by impact

**Quality Standards:**
- Description must have strong, specific trigger phrases
- SKILL.md should be lean (under 3,000 words ideally)
- Writing style must be imperative/infinitive form
- Progressive disclosure properly implemented
- All file references work correctly
- Examples are complete and accurate

**Output Format:**
## Skill Review: [skill-name]

### Summary
[Overall assessment and word counts]

### Description Analysis
**Current:** [Show current description]

**Issues:**
- [Issue 1 with description]
- [Issue 2...]

**Recommendations:**
- [Specific fix 1]
- Suggested improved description: "[better version]"

### Content Quality

**SKILL.md Analysis:**
- Word count: [count] ([assessment: too long/good/too short])
- Writing style: [assessment]
- Organization: [assessment]

**Issues:**
- [Content issue 1]
- [Content issue 2]

**Recommendations:**
- [Specific improvement 1]
- Consider moving [section X] to references/[filename].md

### Progressive Disclosure

**Current Structure:**
- SKILL.md: [word count]
- references/: [count] files, [total words]
- examples/: [count] files
- scripts/: [count] files

**Assessment:**
[Is progressive disclosure effective?]

**Recommendations:**
[Suggestions for better organization]

### Specific Issues

#### Critical ([count])
- [File/location]: [Issue] - [Fix]

#### Major ([count])
- [File/location]: [Issue] - [Recommendation]

#### Minor ([count])
- [File/location]: [Issue] - [Suggestion]

### Positive Aspects
- [What's done well 1]
- [What's done well 2]

### Overall Rating
[Pass/Needs Improvement/Needs Major Revision]

### Priority Recommendations
1. [Highest priority fix]
2. [Second priority]
3. [Third priority]

**Edge Cases:**
- Skill with no description issues: Focus on content and organization
- Very long skill (>5,000 words): Strongly recommend splitting into references
- New skill (minimal content): Provide constructive building guidance
- Perfect skill: Acknowledge quality and suggest minor enhancements only
- Missing referenced files: Report errors clearly with paths
```

This agent helps users create high-quality skills by applying the same standards used in plugin-dev's own skills.

## request_user_input availability

Use the `request_user_input` tool only when it is listed in the available tools for this turn.

Use the `request_user_input` tool only for optional questions where the answer would materially improve the quality of the work.

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` moves them to `raw/sources/clips/` when done |
| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex runs the catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills, so the two skill tables legitimately differ.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 4.2 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"bbefa5","wall_time_seconds":3.9942979,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2804,"output":"---\\r\\nname: grilling\\r\\ndescription: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any \'grill\' trigger phrases.\\r\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\nInterview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.\\r\\n\\r\\nWork the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven\'t heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user\'s answers before the next round.\\r\\n\\r\\nFormat a round like so:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\\r\\n\\r\\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>\\r\\n\\r\\n➡️ <your recommended answer>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nEach round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.\\r\\n\\r\\nFinding _facts_ is your job, never the user\'s. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don\'t ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don\'t block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user\'s: put each to them and wait.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.\\r\\n---\\r\\nname: domain-modeling\\r\\ndescription: Build and sharpen a project\'s domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a CONTEXT.md, or recording or editing an ADR.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Domain Modeling\\r\\n\\r\\nActively build and sharpen the project\'s domain model as you design. This is the *active* discipline: challenging terms, inventing edge-case scenarios, and writing the glossary and decisions down the moment they crystallise. (Merely *reading* `CONTEXT.md` for vocabulary is not this skill: that\'s a one-line habit any skill can do. This skill is for when you\'re changing the model, not just consuming it.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## File structure\\r\\n\\r\\nMost repos have a single context:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n/\\r\\n├── CONTEXT.md\\r\\n├── docs/\\r\\n│   └── adr/\\r\\n│       ├── 0001-event-sourced-orders.md\\r\\n│       └── 0002-postgres-for-write-model.md\\r\\n└── src/\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists at the root, the repo has multiple contexts. The map points to where each one lives:\\r\\n\\r\\n```\\r\\n/\\r\\n├── CONTEXT-MAP.md\\r\\n├── docs/\\r\\n│   └── adr/                          ← system-wide decisions\\r\\n├── src/\\r\\n│   ├── ordering/\\r\\n│   │   ├── CONTEXT.md\\r\\n│   │   └── docs/adr/                 ← context-specific decisions\\r\\n│   └── billing/\\r\\n│       ├── CONTEXT.md\\r\\n│       └── docs/adr/\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate files lazily: only when you have something to write. If no `CONTEXT.md` exists, create one when the first term is resolved. If no `docs/adr/` exists, create it when the first ADR is needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## During the session\\r\\n\\r\\n### Challenge against the glossary\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user uses a term that conflicts with the existing language in `CONTEXT.md`, call it out immediately. \\"Your glossary defines \'cancellation\' as X, but you seem to mean Y. Which is it?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### Sharpen fuzzy language\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user uses vague or overloaded terms, propose a precise canonical term. \\"You\'re saying \'account\': do you mean the Customer or the User? Those are different things.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### Discuss concrete scenarios\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen domain relationships are being discussed, stress-test them with specific scenarios. Invent scenarios that probe edge cases and force the user to be precise about the boundaries between concepts.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Cross-reference with code\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the user states how something works, check whether the code agrees. If you find a contradiction, surface it: \\"Your code cancels entire Orders, but you just said partial cancellation is possible. Which is right?\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### Update CONTEXT.md inline\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a term is resolved, update `CONTEXT.md` right there. Don\'t batch these up: capture them as they happen. Use the format in [CONTEXT-FORMAT.md](./CONTEXT-FORMAT.md).\\r\\n\\r\\n`CONTEXT.md` should be totally devoid of implementation details. Do not treat `CONTEXT.md` as a spec, a scratch pad, or a repository for implementation decisions. It is a glossary and nothing else.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Offer ADRs sparingly\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly offer to create an ADR when all three are true:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Hard to reverse**: the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful\\r\\n2. **Surprising without context**: a future reader will wonder \\"why did they do it this way?\\"\\r\\n3. **The result of a real trade-off**: there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons\\r\\n\\r\\nIf any of the three is missing, skip the ADR. domain-modeling\\r\\nFile          : SKILL.md\\r\\nCatalogExists : True\\r\\nMatches       : True\\r\\n\\r\\nRuntime       : claude\\r\\nSkill         : domain-modeling\\r\\nFile          : agents\\\\openai.yaml\\r\\nCatalogExists : True\\r\\nMatches       : True\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface:\\r\\n  display_name: \\"Grilling\\"\\r\\n  short_description: \\"Stress-test thinking a round of questions at a time\\"\\r\\n# CONTEXT.md Format\\r\\n\\r\\n## Structure\\r\\n\\r\\n```md\\r\\n# {Context Name}\\r\\n\\r\\n{One or two sentence description of what this context is and why it exists.}\\r\\n\\r\\n## Language\\r\\n\\r\\n**Order**:\\r\\n{A one or two sentence description of the term}\\r\\n_Avoid_: Purchase, transaction\\r\\n\\r\\n**Invoice**:\\r\\nA request for payment sent to a customer after delivery.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Bill, payment request\\r\\n\\r\\n**Customer**:\\r\\nA person or organization that places orders.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Client, buyer, account\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Be opinionated.** When multiple words exist for the same concept, pick the best one and list the others under `_Avoid_`.\\r\\n- **Keep definitions tight.** One or two sentences max. Define what it IS, not what it does.\\r\\n- **Only include terms specific to this project\'s context.** General programming concepts (timeouts, error types, utility patterns) don\'t belong even if the project uses them extensively. Before adding a term, ask: is this a concept unique to this context, or a general programming concept? Only the former belongs.\\r\\n- **Group terms under subheadings** when natural clusters emerge. If all terms belong to a single cohesive area, a flat list is fine.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Single vs multi-context repos\\r\\n\\r\\n**Single context (most repos):** One `CONTEXT.md` at the repo root.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Multiple contexts:** A `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the repo root lists the contexts, where they live, and how they relate to each other:\\r\\n\\r\\n```md\\r\\n# Context Map\\r\\n\\r\\n## Contexts\\r\\n\\r\\n- [Ordering](./src/ordering/CONTEXT.md): receives and tracks customer orders\\r\\n- [Billing](./src/billing/CONTEXT.md): generates invoices and processes payments\\r\\n- [Fulfillment](./src/fulfillment/CONTEXT.md): manages warehouse picking and shipping\\r\\n\\r\\n## Relationships\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Ordering → Fulfillment**: Ordering emits `OrderPlaced` events; Fulfillment consumes them to start picking\\r\\n- **Fulfillment → Billing**: Fulfillment emits `ShipmentDispatched` events; Billing consumes them to generate invoices\\r\\n- **Ordering ↔ Billing**: Shared types for `CustomerId` and `Money`\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe skill infers which structure applies:\\r\\n\\r\\n- If `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists, read it to find contexts\\r\\n- If only a root `CONTEXT.md` exists, single context\\r\\n- If neither exists, create a root `CONTEXT.md` lazily when the first term is resolved\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen multiple contexts exist, infer which one the current topic relates to. If unclear, ask.\\r\\n# ADR Format\\r\\n\\r\\nADRs live in `docs/adr/` and use sequential numbering: `0001-slug.md`, `0002-slug.md`, etc.\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate the `docs/adr/` directory lazily: only when the first ADR is needed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Template\\r\\n\\r\\n```md\\r\\n# {Short title of the decision}\\r\\n\\r\\n{1-3 sentences: what\'s the context, what did we decide, and why.}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThat\'s it. An ADR can be a single paragraph. The value is in recording *that* a decision was made and *why*, not in filling out sections.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Optional sections\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly include these when they add genuine value. Most ADRs won\'t need them.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Status** frontmatter (`proposed | accepted | deprecated | superseded by ADR-NNNN`): useful when decisions are revisited\\r\\n- **Considered Options**: only when the rejected alternatives are worth remembering\\r\\n- **Consequences**: only when non-obvious downstream effects need to be called out\\r\\n\\r\\n## Numbering\\r\\n\\r\\nScan `docs/adr/` for the highest existing number and increment by one.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When to offer an ADR\\r\\n\\r\\nAll three of these must be true:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Hard to reverse**: the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful\\r\\n2. **Surprising without context**: a future reader will look at the code and wonder \\"why on earth did they do it this way?\\"\\r\\n3. **The result of a real trade-off**: there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a decision is easy to reverse, skip it: you\'ll just reverse it. If it\'s not surprising, nobody will wonder why. If there was no real alternative, there\'s nothing to record beyond \\"we did the obvious thing.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### What qualifies\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Architectural shape.** \\"We\'re using a monorepo.\\" \\"The write model is event-sourced, the read model is projected into Postgres.\\"\\r\\n- **Integration patterns between contexts.** \\"Ordering and Billing communicate via domain events, not synchronous HTTP.\\"\\r\\n- **Technology choices that carry lock-in.** Database, message bus, auth provider, deployment target. Not every library: just the ones that would take a quarter to swap out.\\r\\n- **Boundary and scope decisions.** \\"Customer data is owned by the Customer context; other contexts reference it by ID only.\\" The explicit no-s are as valuable as the yes-s.\\r\\n- **Deliberate deviations from the obvious path.** \\"We\'re using manual SQL instead of an ORM because X.\\" Anything where a reasonable reader would assume the opposite. These stop the next engineer from \\"fixing\\" something that was deliberate.\\r\\n- **Constraints not visible in the code.** \\"We can\'t use AWS because of compliance requirements.\\" \\"Response times must be under 200ms because of the partner API contract.\\"\\r\\n- **Rejected alternatives when the rejection is non-obvious.** If you considered GraphQL and picked REST for subtle reasons, record it; otherwise someone will suggest GraphQL again in six months.\\r\\n"}'}]

Two material issues remain:

1. **Claude dependency loading:** `.claude/skills/grill-with-docs/SKILL.md` prefers invoking `grilling` through the Skill tool, but `grilling` has `disable-model-invocation: true`. Read sibling files directly, or explicitly fall back when tool invocation is prohibited or fails.

2. **Conflicting documentation timing:** `grilling` ends with “Do not act on it until the user confirms,” while the wrapper requires updating documents as answers arrive. Clarify in the wrapper that recording settled answers happens during the interview; implementation waits for shared understanding.

Verified: all inspected active files match their respective runtime catalogs; sibling links and domain templates exist; the wrapper preserves settled user decisions and corrections; Codex wrapper metadata disables implicit invocation correctly.

items: ActivityItem[] };\\r\\n  position: number;\\r\\n  stage: \\"intro\\" | \\"paper\\" | \\"choices\\" | \\"feedback\\" | \\"done\\";\\r\\n  selection: string;\\r\\n  answers: PracticeAnswer[];\\r\\n}\\r\\ninterface CachedRun { run: PracticeRun; revision: number | null; dirty: boolean }\\r\\ninterface RunRow { id: string; user_id: string; snapshot: Pick<PracticeRun, \\"mode\\" | \\"questions\\" | \\"general\\" | \\"createdAt\\">; state: Omit<PracticeRun, \\"id\\" | \\"userId\\" | \\"mode\\" | \\"questions\\" | \\"general\\" | \\"createdAt\\">; revision: number }\\r\\nconst prefix = \\"lsatu:conditional-practice:v1:\\";\\r\\nconst keyFor = (userId: string, id: string) => `${prefix}${userId}:${id}`;\\r\\n// This feature owns its row shape until generated database types are refreshed.\\r\\nconst table = () => (supabase as unknown as { from: (name: string) => ReturnType<typeof supabase.from> }).from(\\"conditional_practice_runs\\");\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function readCachedRuns(userId: string): PracticeRun[] {\\r\\n  const runs: PracticeRun[] = [];\\r\\n  for (let i = 0; i < localStorage.length; i++) {\\r\\n    const key = localStorage.key(i);\\r\\n    if (!key?.startsWith(`${prefix}${userId}:`)) continue;\\r\\n    const cached = readCache(userId, key.slice(`${prefix}${userId}:`.length));\\r\\n    if (cached) runs.push(cached.run);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return runs.sort((a, b) => b.createdAt.localeCompare(a.createdAt));\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction readCache(userId: string, id: string): CachedRun | null {\\r\\n  const raw = localStorage.getItem(keyFor(userId, id));\\r\\n  if (!raw) return null;\\r\\n  const cached = JSON.parse(raw) as CachedRun;\\r\\n  if (cached.run?.userId !== userId || cached.run.id !== id || !Array.isArray(cached.run.answers)) {\\r\\n    throw new Error(\\"A saved practice session could not be read. Its stored copy has been kept.\\");\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return cached;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction writeCache(cached: CachedRun) {\\r\\n  localStorage.setItem(keyFor(cached.run.userId, cached.run.id), JSON.stringify(cached));\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport function cachePracticeRun(run: PracticeRun): void {\\r\\n  const previous = readCache(run.userId, run.id);\\r\\n  writeCache({ run, revision: previous?.revision ?? null, dirty: true });\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport function createPracticeRun(userId: string, mode: PracticeMode, questions: ConditionalQuestion[], general?: PracticeRun[\\"general\\"]): PracticeRun {\\r\\n  const now = new Date().toISOString();\\r\\n  const run: PracticeRun = { id: crypto.randomUUID(), userId, mode, createdAt: now, updatedAt: now,\\r\\n    questions: structuredClone(questions), general: general ? structuredClone(general) : undefined,\\r\\n    position: 0, stage: \\"paper\\", selection: \\"\\", answers: [] };\\r\\n  cachePracticeRun(run);\\r\\n  return run;\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction toRow(run: PracticeRun) {\\r\\n  const { id, userId, mode, questions, general, createdAt, ...state } = run;\\r\\n  return { id, user_id: userId, snapshot: { mode, questions, ...(general ? { general } : {}), createdAt }, state };\\r\\n}\\r\\nfunction fromRow(row: RunRow): PracticeRun {\\r\\n  return { id: row.id, userId: row.user_id, ...row.snapshot, ...row.state };\\r\\n}\\r\\nconst uploads = new Map<string, Promise<void>>();\\r\\nexport function syncPracticeRun(userId: string, id: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  const key = keyFor(userId, id);\\r\\n  const existing = uploads.get(key);\\r\\n  if (existing) return existing;\\r\\n  const upload = uploadPending(userId, id).finally(() => uploads.delete(key));\\r\\n  uploads.set(key, upload);\\r\\n  return upload;\\r\\n}\\r\\nasync function uploadPending(userId: string, id: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  for (;;) {\\r\\n    const outgoing = readCache(userId, id);\\r\\n    if (!outgoing?.dirty) return;\\r\\n    const row = toRow(outgoing.run);\\r\\n    let revision: number;\\r\\n    if (outgoing.revision === null) {\\r\\n      const { error } = await table().insert({ ...row, revision: 0 } as never);\\r\\n      if (error) {\\r\\n        if (error.code !== \\"23505\\") throw error;\\r\\n        // A response can be lost after a successful insert. Only accept that\\r\\n        // retry when the remote record is exactly the snapshot being uploaded.\\r\\n        const remote = await table().select(\\"*\\").eq(\\"id\\", id).eq(\\"user_id\\", userId).single();\\r\\n        if (remote.error) throw remote.error;\\r\\n        const remoteRun = fromRow(remote.data as unknown as RunRow);\\r\\n        if (JSON.stringify(toRow(remoteRun)) !== JSON.stringify(row)) throw new Error(\\"This session changed in another tab or device. Your device copy is kept; reload before continuing.\\");\\r\\n        revision = (remote.data as unknown as RunRow).revision;\\r\\n      } else revision = 0;\\r\\n    } else {\\r\\n      revision = outgoing.revision + 1;\\r\\n      const result = await table().update({ state: row.state, revision } as never)\\r\\n        .eq(\\"id\\", id).eq(\\"user_id\\", userId).eq(\\"revision\\", outgoing.revision).select(\\"id\\");\\r\\n      if (result.error) throw result.error;\\r\\n      if (!result.data?.length) throw new Error(\\"This session changed in another tab or device. Your device copy is kept; reload before continuing.\\");\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    const latest = readCache(userId, id);\\r\\n    if (!latest) return;\\r\\n    writeCache({ run: latest.run, revision, dirty: JSON.stringify(latest.run) !== JSON.stringify(outgoing.run) });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport async function loadPracticeRuns(userId: string): Promise<PracticeRun[]> {\\r\\n  const { data, error } = await table().select(\\"*\\").eq(\\"user_id\\", userId).order(\\"created_at\\", { ascending: false });\\r\\n  if (error) throw error;\\r\\n  for (const row of (data ?? []) as unknown as RunRow[]) {\\r\\n    const local = readCache(userId, row.id);\\r\\n    if (!local?.dirty) writeCache({ run: fromRow(row), revision: row.revision, dirty: false });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return readCachedRuns(userId);\\r\\n}\\r\\nexport async function syncPendingPractice(userId: string): Promise<void> {\\r\\n  for (const run of readCachedRuns(userId)) await syncPracticeRun(userId, run.id);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function practiceSaveMessage(error: unknown): string {\\r\\n  const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String((error as { message?: string })?.message ?? \\"Connection unavailable\\");\\r\\n  if (message.includes(\\"another tab\\")) return message;\\r\\n  return \\"Saved on this device. Account sync is unavailable; keep using this device and retry syncing when connected.\\";\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project Map\\r\\n\\r\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\r\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\r\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\r\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\r\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\r\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\r\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\r\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Stack And Commands\\r\\n\\r\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\r\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\r\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\r\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\r\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\r\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\r\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\r\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\r\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\n## Working Rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\r\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\r\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\r\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\r\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\r\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\r\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\r\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product Constraints\\r\\n\\r\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\r\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\r\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\r\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\r\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Smart Drill Note\\r\\n\\r\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Workflow Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\r\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\r\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\r\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\r\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\r\\n\\r\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\r\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\r\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Known Gaps\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\r\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\r\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. \\"Preview: saved on this device\\" : \\"Loading saved practice…\\");\\r\\n  const [mode, setMode] = useState<ConditionalMode | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [level, setLevel] = useState<\\"all\\" | \\"variables\\" | \\"words\\">(\\"all\\");\\r\\n  const [reviewId, setReviewId] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const alive = useRef(true);\\r\\n  const syncCount = useRef(0);\\r\\n  const run = runs.find(r => r.id === params.get(\\"run\\"));\\r\\n  const selectedWorld = WORLDS.find(w => w.id === mode);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const refresh = useCallback(async () => {\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      setRuns(readCachedRuns(userId));\\r\\n      setLoaded(true);\\r\\n    } catch (error) {\\r\\n      if (alive.current) { setLoaded(true); setStorageError(error instanceof Error ? error.message : \\"Could not read saved practice on this device.\\"); }\\r\\n      return;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    if (localOnly) return;\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      await syncPendingPractice(userId);\\r\\n      const saved = await loadPracticeRuns(userId);\\r\\n      if (alive.current) { setRuns(saved); setSaveStatus(\\"Saved to your account\\"); }\\r\\n    } catch (error) {\\r\\n      if (alive.current) setSaveStatus(practiceSaveMessage(error));\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [userId, localOnly]);\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    alive.current = true;\\r\\n    void refresh();\\r\\n    const online = () => void refresh();\\r\\n    window.addEventListener(\\"online\\", online);\\r\\n    return () => { alive.current = false; window.removeEventListener(\\"online\\", online); };\\r\\n  }, [refresh]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const persist = (next: PracticeRun): boolean => {\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      cachePracticeRun(next);\\r\\n      setRuns(previous => [next, ...previous.filter(r => r.id !== next.id)].sort((a, b) => b.createdAt.localeCompare(a.createdAt)));\\r\\n      setStorageError(null);\\r\\n    } catch {\\r\\n      setStorageError(\\"This browser could not save your answer. CONDITIONAL_QUESTIONS.filter(q => q.mode === world && (level === \\"all\\" || q.level === level));\\r\\n    if (!selected.length) return;\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const next = createPracticeRun(userId, world, selected);\\r\\n      if (persist(next)) { setParams({ run: next.id }); setMode(null); setReviewId(null); }\\r\\n    } catch { setStorageError(\\"This browser cannot save a new session. run.answers.filter(a => a.questionId === current.id) : [];\\r\\n  const last = questionAnswers.at(-1);\\r\\n  const resolved = !!last && (last.verdict === \\"correct\\" || last.revealed || questionAnswers.length >= 2);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const submit = (reveal = false) => {\\r\\n    if (!run || !current || (!run.selection && !reveal) || run.stage === \\"feedback\\" && !reveal) return;\\r\\n    const selected = current.choices.find(c => c.id === run.selection);\\r\\n    const answer = { id: crypto.randomUUID(), questionId: current.id, choiceId: selected?.id,\\r\\n      text: reveal ? \\"Answer revealed\\" : selected!.text,\\r\\n      verdict: reveal ? null : selected!.id === current.correctChoiceId ? \\"correct\\" as const : \\"incorrect\\" as const,\\r\\n      attempt: questionAnswers.length + 1, revealed: reveal, at: new Date().toISOString() };\\r\\n    update({ answers: [...run.answers, answer], stage: \\"feedback\\" });\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <main className=\\"conditional-room activity-centre\\">\\r\\n    <div className=\\"conditional-shell\\">\\r\\n      <header className=\\"conditional-topbar\\">\\r\\n        {run || mode || review ? <button onClick={returnToHub} className=\\"conditional-back\\"><ArrowLeft size={16} /> Conditional worlds</button>\\r\\n          : <Link to=\\"/activities\\" className=\\"conditional-back\\"><ArrowLeft size={16} /> Activities</Link>}\\r\\n        <div className=\\"conditional-save\\" role=\\"status\\">{saveStatus}\\r\\n          {!localOnly && saveStatus.includes(\\"unavailable\\") && <button onClick={() => void refresh()}>Retry sync</button>}</div>\\r\\n      </header>\\r\\n      {storageError && <p role=\\"alert\\" className=\\"conditional-error\\">{storageError}</p>}\\r\\n      {!loaded ? <PracticeReview run={review} onBack={() => setReviewId(null)} />\\r\\n      : run && run.stage === \\"done\\" ? <section className=\\"conditional-panel conditional-summary\\">\\r\\n          <span className=\\"conditional-eyebrow\\">{titleFor(run.mode)}</span><h1>Practice complete</h1>\\r\\n          <p>{firstTryCorrect(run)} of {run.questions.length} correct on the first answer. Corrections and reveals are kept separately.</p>\\r\\n          <div className=\\"conditional-actions\\"><button className=\\"conditional-primary\\" onClick={() => setReviewId(run.id)}>Review answers</button>\\r\\n          <button onClick={() => start(run.mode as ConditionalMode, run.questions)}>Practice again</button><button onClick={returnToHub}>Choose a world</button></div>\\r\\n        </section>\\r\\n      : run && current ? <>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"conditional-heading\\"><span className=\\"conditional-eyebrow\\">{titleFor(run.mode)}</span><span>Question {run.position + 1} of {run.questions.length}</span></div>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"conditional-progress\\"><span style={{ width: `${run.position / run.questions.length * 100}%` }} /></div>\\r\\n        <div className=\\"conditional-question-grid\\">\\r\\n          <section className=\\"conditional-premises\\"><span className=\\"conditional-eyebrow\\">Given</span>\\r\\n            {current.premises.map((p, i) => <div className=\\"conditional-premise\\" key={i}><span className=\\"conditional-premise-number\\">{i + 1}</span><p><ConditionalNotation text={p} /></p></div>)}\\r\\n            {current.conclusion && <div className=\\"conditional-target\\"><span className=\\"conditional-eyebrow\\">Conclusion</span><p><ConditionalNotation text={current.conclusion} /></p></div>}\\r\\n            <p className=\\"conditional-paper-note\\">Diagram the relationships on paper before opening the choices.</p>\\r\\n          </section>\\r\\n          <section className=\\"conditional-panel conditional-answer\\"><h1>{run.mode === \\"must-be-true\\" ? \\"selected\\" : \\"\\"}`}>\\r\\n                <input type=\\"radio\\" name={`answer-${current.id}`} checked={run.selection === choice.id} disabled={run.stage === \\"feedback\\"} onChange={() => update({ selection: choice.id })} />\\r\\n                <span className=\\"conditional-choice-letter\\" aria-hidden=\\"true\\">{String.fromCharCode(65 + i)}</span><span><ConditionalNotation text={choice.text} /></span>\\r\\n              </label>)}</div>\\r\\n              {run.stage === \\"choices\\" && <button className=\\"conditional-primary\\" disabled={!run.selection} onClick={() => submit()}>Check answer</button>}\\r\\n              {run.stage === \\"feedback\\" && last && <div role=\\"status\\" className=\\"conditional-feedback\\">\\r\\n                <h2>{last.revealed ? \\"What follows\\" : \\"The missing link\\"}</span><p><ConditionalNotation text={current.choices.find(c => c.id === current.correctChoiceId)!.text} /></p></div>\\r\\n                  <p className=\\"conditional-explanation\\"><ConditionalNotation text={current.explanation} /></p>\\r\\n                  <button className=\\"conditional-primary\\" onClick={() => update(run.position + 1 === run.questions.length ? { stage: \\"done\\" } : { position: run.position + 1, stage: \\"paper\\", selection: \\"\\" })}>{run.position + 1 === run.questions.length ? \\"Finish practice\\" : \\"Next question\\"}<ArrowRight size={16} /></button></>\\r\\n                  : <><p>{run.mode === \\"must-be-true\\" ? \\"Check which conditions the facts establish. The result is A → B → C → D.\\"}</p></div>\\r\\n        <label className=\\"conditional-select\\">Practice with <select value={level} onChange={e => setLevel(e.target.value as typeof level)}><option value=\\"all\\">All questions, in progression</option><option value=\\"variables\\">Variables</option><option value=\\"words\\">Words and phrases</option></select></label>\\r\\n        <p>{CONDITIONAL_QUESTIONS.filter(q => q.mode === mode && (level === \\"all\\" || q.level === level)).length} questions. <>{generalUrl ? <button className=\\"conditional-primary\\" onClick={() => navigate(generalUrl)}>Open original drill <ArrowRight size={16} /></button> : <p className=\\"conditional-unavailable\\">{generalStatus}</p>}\\r\\n              {unfinished?.general && <button onClick={() => navigate(`${unfinished.general.sourceUrl}?practiceRun=${unfinished.id}`)}>Continue saved practice</button>}</>\\r\\n              : <><button className=\\"conditional-primary\\" onClick={() => { setMode(world.id); setLevel(\\"all\\"); }}>Choose practice <ArrowRight size={16} /></button>\\r\\n                {unfinished && <button onClick={() => setParams({ run: unfinished.id })}>Continue question {unfinished.position + 1}</button>}</>}\\r\\n          </section>;\\r\\n        })}</div>\\r\\n        <section className=\\"conditional-history\\"><h2><History size={20} /> Your practice history</h2><p>First answers, corrections, and revealed answers stay with their session.</p>\\r\\n          {runs.length === 0 ? <p>No practice sessions yet.</p> : runs.map(saved => <div className=\\"conditional-history-row\\" key={saved.id}><div><strong>{titleFor(saved.mode)}</strong><span>{saved.createdAt.slice(0, 10)} · {saved.stage === \\"done\\" ? \\"Completed\\" : \\"In progress\\"} · {saved.answers.length} recorded responses</span></div>\\r\\n            <button onClick={() => saved.general ? navigate(`${saved.general.sourceUrl}?practiceRun=${saved.id}`) : saved.stage === \\"done\\" ? Conditional practice has its own saved\\r\\n * session and never touches the original graded score.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface ActivityRunnerProps {\\r\\n  /** When embedded (not routed), the assignment id to run. Falls back to the URL param. */\\r\\n  assignmentId?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Teacher preview starts at the intro and never changes assignment scores.\\r\\n   * Own General Conditional previews save a separate practice session.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  preview?: boolean;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * A run is one mounted player.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The Report Card\'s \\"Run these again\\" navigates to a NEW assignment id under\\r\\n * the same `/activities/:assignmentId` route, so React keeps this component\\r\\n * mounted and every piece of run state -- the phase, the hydrated flag, the\\r\\n * attempt-log promise -- would carry over from the finished run and leave her\\r\\n * staring at the grading screen of a run that has no recording. Keying on the\\r\\n * id makes a new run a new mount, which is what it actually is.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function ActivityRunner(props: ActivityRunnerProps = {}) {\\r\\n  const params = useParams<{ assignmentId: string; activityId: string }>();\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n  const key = `${props.assignmentId ?? params.assignmentId ?? params.activityId ?? \\"none\\"}:${new URLSearchParams(location.search).get(\\"practiceRun\\") ?? \\"\\"}`;\\r\\n  return <ActivityRunnerInstance key={key} {...props} />;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ActivityRunnerInstance({ assignmentId: assignmentIdProp, preview = false }: ActivityRunnerProps = {}) {\\r\\n  const params = useParams<{ assignmentId: string; activityId: string }>();\\r\\n  // The routed preview names an ACTIVITY, not an assignment: it runs off the\\r\\n  // definition and no row exists to name. Everything else names an assignment.\\r\\n  const previewActivityId = preview ? params.activityId : undefined;\\r\\n  const assignmentId = assignmentIdProp ?? params.assignmentId;\\r\\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\\r\\n  const location = useLocation();\\r\\n  const requestedPracticeId = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get(\\"practiceRun\\");\\r\\n  const [savedPractice, setSavedPractice] = useState<PracticeRun | null>(null);\\r\\n  const savedPracticeRef = useRef<PracticeRun | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [practiceError, setPracticeError] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [saveStatus, setSaveStatus] = useState(\\"\\");\\r\\n  const startSectionOnOpen =\\r\\n    !preview &&\\r\\n    typeof location.state === \\"object\\" &&\\r\\n    location.state !== null &&\\r\\n    \\"startSection\\" in location.state &&\\r\\n    location.state.startSection === true;\\r\\n  // Only the routed runner owns the whole viewport and therefore needs its own\\r\\n  // way out. Embedded (teacher preview) sits inside chrome that already closes.\\r\\n  const routed = assignmentIdProp === undefined;\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Exit means two different places depending on where you are standing, and\\r\\n  // conflating them is what made the first version wrong. From a question, the\\r\\n  // thing you want to back out to is the Activity\'s own intro: its title, the\\r\\n  // worked example, the rules. Dropping straight to the Activity Centre there\\r\\n  // skips past the page you were actually trying to reach. From the intro\\r\\n  // itself there is nowhere further in to go, so that one leaves for the\\r\\n  // Centre. Returning to the intro keeps `index`, so the Start action resumes on\\r\\n  // the same question rather than restarting the Activity.\\r\\n  const handleBackToIntro = routed ? () => setPhase(\\"intro\\") : undefined;\\r\\n  const handleLeave = routed ? () => navigate(isGeneral ? \\"/activities/conditionals\\" : \\"/activities\\") : undefined;\\r\\n  const { user } = useAuth();\\r\\n\\r\\n  // \\"Activity not found.\\" was shown for two very different situations: an id\\r\\n  // that is not real, and an assignment that IS real but was issued to another\\r\\n  // account. The second is the common one for anyone signed in as admin while\\r\\n  // the assignment sits on their student login, and the old wording sent the\\r\\n  // reader looking for a routing or RLS bug that was never there.\\r\\n  // Tri-state, not a boolean. A boolean starting false means the first paint\\r\\n  // after an empty load says \\"Activity not found.\\" and only corrects itself\\r\\n  // once the probe answers, so the wrong sentence is shown first and the right\\r\\n  // one arrives as a flicker. \\"unknown\\" keeps the spinner up instead.\\r\\n  const [ownership, setOwnership] = useState<\\"unknown\\" | \\"elsewhere\\" | \\"absent\\">(\\"unknown\\");\\r\\n  const { assignment, loading, error } = useStudentActivity(assignmentId, previewActivityId);\\r\\n\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Joshua, opening a link he handed to a student.\\r\\n   *\\r\\n   * An admin can read every assignment, so the row loads; what must not happen\\r\\n   * is his visit being recorded as the student\'s work. Every write below is\\r\\n   * gated on `readOnly` rather than `practice` alone, and the attempt log is\\r\\n   * read under the assignment\'s OWN student id, so what he sees on the screen\\r\\n   * is their answers rather than an empty run.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  const viewingSomeoneElse = Boolean(assignment && user && assignment.student_id !== user.id);\\r\\n  const isGeneral = assignment?.title === \\"Conditional Rephrase\\";\\r\\n  const persistPractice = useCallback((next: PracticeRun): boolean => {\\r\\n    if (!user || next.userId !== user.id || viewingSomeoneElse) return false;\\r\\n    try { cachePracticeRun(next); }\\r\\n    catch (error) { setPracticeError(error instanceof Error ? error.message : \\"This browser could not save your practice. Retry before continuing.\\"); return false; }\\r\\n    savedPracticeRef.current = next;\\r\\n    setSavedPractice(next);\\r\\n    setPracticeError(null);\\r\\n    setSaveStatus(\\"Saved on this device; syncing...\\");\\r\\n    void syncPracticeRun(user.id, next.id).then(() => setSaveStatus(\\"Saved to your account\\")).catch(error => {\\r\\n      const message = practiceSaveMessage(error);\\r\\n      setSaveStatus(message);\\r\\n      toast({ title: \\"Practice account sync unavailable\\", description: message });\\r\\n    });\\r\\n    return true;\\r\\n  }, [user, viewingSomeoneElse]);\\r\\n  const updatePractice = useCallback((change: Partial<PracticeRun>) => {\\r\\n    const run = savedPracticeRef.current;\\r\\n    return run ? persistPractice({ ...run, ...change, updatedAt: new Date().toISOString() }) : false;\\r\\n  }, [persistPractice]);\\r\\n  const logStudentId = assignment?.student_id ?? user?.id;\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Only asked when the load has genuinely come back empty, so the ordinary\\r\\n  // path costs nothing. It used to end as\\r\\n      // \\"incorrect\\", which put a red verdict on an answer nobody had read and\\r\\n      // then scored it as a zero for the rest of time.\\r\\n      toast({\\r\\n        title: \\"Coach unavailable\\",\\r\\n        description: \\"Showing the credited answer so you can keep moving. This one is not counted.\\",\\r\\n        variant: \\"destructive\\",\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      record({\\r\\n        itemOrder: item.order,\\r\\n        responseText: value,\\r\\n        verdict: null,\\r\\n        attemptNo,\\r\\n        judgeSource: \\"fallback\\",\\r\\n        shadowFault: shadowFault?.(item, value) ?? null,\\r\\n        latencyMs: since(),\\r\\n      });\\r\\n      setResult({ verdict: \\"unchecked\\", feedback: \\"\\" });\\r\\n      resolve(null, true);\\r\\n      return;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n\\r\\n    const verdict = res.verdict.verdict;\\r\\n    setResult(res.verdict);\\r\\n    record({\\r\\n      itemOrder: item.order,\\r\\n      responseText: value,\\r\\n      verdict,\\r\\n      attemptNo,\\r\\n      judgeSource: \\"coach\\",\\r\\n      shadowFault: shadowFault?.(item, value) ?? null,\\r\\n      latencyMs: since(),\\r\\n    });\\r\\n\\r\\n    if (verdict === \\"correct\\") {\\r\\n      resolve(\\"correct\\", false);\\r\\n    } else if (attemptNo >= MAX_ATTEMPTS) {\\r\\n      resolve(verdict, true);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    // else: leave unresolved so the Try again / Show answer affordances show.\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Try again used to empty the box. With a twenty-word answer and a one-word\\r\\n   * miss that is a punishment for being close: the student retypes nineteen\\r\\n   * words they had right. The text stays and arrives selected, so starting over\\r\\n   * is still one keystroke away.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  const handleRetry = () => {\\r\\n    setResult(null);\\r\\n    requestAnimationFrame(() => {\\r\\n      const box = answerRef.current;\\r\\n      if (!box) return;\\r\\n      box.focus();\\r\\n      box.select();\\r\\n    });\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Bailing out to the credited answer ends the item, so the log has to say so.\\r\\n   * It used to write nothing: the replay then saw one sub-cap attempt, called\\r\\n   * the item unresolved, and served it again on the next sitting as though the\\r\\n   * student had never reached it.
